Links of type II over a curve:

\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{X_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{X_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{2}}
\textstyle{C,}

where
X_{1},X_{2}
are
G
-Mori conic bundles over
C
and
Z
is their blow-up at
G
-orbits.

The fibration
X_{1}\to C

may have singular fibres, and
p_{1}\colon Z\to X_{1}
is the blow-up of some orbit
\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\}
consisting of points in distinct non-singular fibres. Let
E_{1},\ldots,E_{n}\subset Z
be the exceptional divisors of
p_{1}
, and let
E^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,E^{\prime}_{n}
be the strict transforms of the corresponding fibres of
f_{1}
. Then
p_{2}\colon Z\to X_{2}
is the blow-down of
E^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,E^{\prime}_{n}
.

We first show that the two SOD-s

(A.23)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(Z)=\left\langle\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}_{E_{1}}(-1)\\
\ldots\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{E_{n}}(-1)\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,p_{1}^{*}(\ker f_{1*}),p_{1}^{*}f_{1}^{*}{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(C)\right\rangle,

(A.24)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(Z)=\left\langle\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}_{1}}(-1)\\
\ldots\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}_{n}}(-1)\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,p_{2}^{*}(\ker f_{2*}),p_{2}^{*}f_{2}^{*}{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(C)\right\rangle

are mutation-equivalent.
For this, note first that
p_{1}^{*}f_{1}^{*}{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(C)=p_{2}^{*}f_{2}^{*}{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(C)=g^{*}{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(C)
since
g=f_{1}p_{1}=f_{2}p_{2}
.
We will use that the left mutation satisfies

L_{g^{*}{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(C)}({\mathcal{O}}_{E_{i}})\cong{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}_{i}}(-1)[1],

which is true since there is an exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}_{i}}(-1)\to{\mathcal{O}}_{E_{i}\cup E^{\prime}_{i}}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{E_{i}}\to 0

and
{\mathcal{O}}_{E_{i}\cup E^{\prime}_{i}}\cong g^{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{pt})\in g^{*}{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(C)
holds (recall that
g^{*}
denotes the derived pull-back).
Now, the following mutations yield the claim:

(A.25)

\text{\eqref{eq_EECC1}}\overset{-K}{\leadsto}\left\langle p_{1}^{*}(\ker f_{1*}),g^{*}{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(C),\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}_{E_{1}}\\
\ldots\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{E_{n}}\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle\right\rangle\overset{L_{3}}{\leadsto}\left\langle p_{1}^{*}(\ker f_{1*}),\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}_{1}}(-1)\\
\ldots\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}_{n}}(-1)\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,g^{*}{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(C)\right\rangle\overset{R_{1}}{\leadsto}\text{\eqref{eq_EECC2}}

Therefore, in the case when
X
is
not
G
-birationally rich
, the statement follows directly because the two SOD-s (
A.23
) and (
A.24
) are exactly the atomic SOD-s

\langle{\mathcal{K}}(p_{i}),p_{i}^{*}{{\mathcal{B}}^{\rm std}}(X_{i}/C)\rangle
for
i=1,2
.

Assume now that
X
is
G
-birationally rich
. In this case the components
p_{i}^{*}(\ker f_{i*})
of
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(Z)
(
i=1,2
) split into two atoms (see Definition
4.21
) and we should take extra care of them. There are three cases to consider: degree
5,6,8
.

Degree of
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
is
5
.

The pull-back of the standard atomic SOD-s for
X_{1}/{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
,
X_{2}/{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
to
Z
are

(A.26)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(Z)=\left\langle\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}_{E_{1}}(-1)\\
\ldots\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{E_{n}}(-1)\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{E}}\rangle,\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{1})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{2})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{3})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{4})\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{5})\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}\rangle\right\rangle,

(A.27)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(Z)=\left\langle\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}_{1}}(-1)\\
\ldots\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}_{n}}(-1)\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\left\langle{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}\right\rangle,\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}(-h^{\prime}_{1})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h^{\prime}_{2})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h^{\prime}_{3})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h^{\prime}_{4})\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}(-h^{\prime}_{5})\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}\rangle\right\rangle,

where fibration
X_{1}\to{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
,
X_{2}\to{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
is given by the divisors
h_{5}
,
h^{\prime}_{5}
, which coincide when we pull them back to
Z
, and the two last components generate
f^{*}{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1})
. The first two mutations from (
A.25
)
transform (
A.26
) into

(A.28)

\left\langle\langle{\mathcal{E}}\rangle,\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{1})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{2})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{3})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{4})\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}_{1}}(-1)\\
\ldots\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}_{n}}(-1)\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{5})\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}\rangle\right\rangle.

Denote
E^{\prime}:=\sum_{j=1}^{n}E^{\prime}_{j}
, then the right mutation
R_{1}R_{2}
takes (
A.28
) to

(A.29)

\left\langle\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}_{1}}(-1)\\
\ldots\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}_{n}}(-1)\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{E}}^{+}\rangle,\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{1}-E^{\prime})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{2}-E^{\prime})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{3}-E^{\prime})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{4}-E^{\prime})\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{5})\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}\rangle\right\rangle,

where
{\mathcal{E}}^{+}
is a vector bundle of rank two. Now we are left to check that some mutations of the pair

(A.30)

\langle{\mathcal{E}}^{+}\rangle,\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{1}-E^{\prime})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{2}-E^{\prime})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{3}-E^{\prime})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{4}-E^{\prime})\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle

is

(A.31)

\langle{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}\rangle,\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}(-h^{\prime}_{1})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h^{\prime}_{2})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h^{\prime}_{3})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h^{\prime}_{4})\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle.

It is easy to compute that (
A.30
) and (
A.31
) are strong exceptional collections, that is, the Ext-groups between objects in the collections are concentrated in degree zero.
Furthermore their endomorphism algebras are isomorphic to the path algebra of the affine quiver
{\tilde{D}}_{4}
of type
D_{4}
(see Figure
1
). It follows using
[
15
, §5, §6]
that the category
{\mathcal{T}}
generated by (
A.30
) (or (
A.31
)) is equivalent to
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\modd{\Bbbk}{\tilde{D}}_{4})
. Let
\phi
(resp.
\phi^{\prime}
) be the equivalence

{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\modd{\Bbbk}{\tilde{D}}_{4})\to{\mathcal{T}}
sending indecomposable projective modules to objects of (
A.30
) (resp. of (
A.31
)). Then
(\phi^{\prime})^{-1}\phi
is an autoequivalence of
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\modd{\Bbbk}{\tilde{D}}_{4})
. They are described in
[
65
]
, in particular, up to some iteration of the Serre functor and a shift,
(\phi^{\prime})^{-1}\phi
sends indecomposable projective modules to indecomposable projective modules. It follows that some iteration
S^{N}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
,
N\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, of the Serre functor on
{\mathcal{T}}
sends (
A.30
) to (
A.31
):

S_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{N}\left(\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{1}-E^{\prime})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{2}-E^{\prime})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{3}-E^{\prime})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{4}-E^{\prime})\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle\right)=\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}(-h^{\prime}_{1})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h^{\prime}_{2})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h^{\prime}_{3})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h^{\prime}_{4})\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle.

It follows that (
A.30
) mutates to (
A.31
) as needed by

L_{2}^{2N}
, see (
3.4
).

Degree of
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
is
6
.

The pull-back of the standard atomic SOD-s for
X_{1}/{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
,
X_{2}/{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
to
Z
are

(A.32)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(Z)=\left\langle\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}_{E_{1}}(-1)\\
\ldots\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{E_{n}}(-1)\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}(-H_{1})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-H_{2})\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{1})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{2})\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{3})\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}\rangle\right\rangle,

(A.33)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(Z)=\left\langle\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}_{1}}(-1)\\
\ldots\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}_{n}}(-1)\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}(-H^{\prime}_{1})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-H^{\prime}_{2})\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}(-h^{\prime}_{1})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h^{\prime}_{2})\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}(-h^{\prime}_{3})\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}\rangle\right\rangle,

where fibrations
X_{1}\to{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
,
X_{2}\to{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
are given by the divisors
h_{3}
,
h^{\prime}_{3}
, which coincide when we lift them to
Z
, and two last components generate
f^{*}{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1})
. First two mutations from (
A.25
)
transform (
A.32
) into

(A.34)

\left\langle\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}(-H_{1})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-H_{2})\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{1})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{2})\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}_{1}}(-1)\\
\ldots\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}_{n}}(-1)\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{3})\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}\rangle\right\rangle.

Denote
E^{\prime}:=\sum_{j=1}^{n}E^{\prime}_{j}
, then the right mutation
R_{1}R_{2}
takes (
A.34
) to

\left\langle\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}_{1}}(-1)\\
\ldots\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}_{n}}(-1)\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}(-H_{1}-E^{\prime})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-H_{2}-E^{\prime})\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{1}-E^{\prime})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{2}-E^{\prime})\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{3})\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}\rangle\right\rangle.

We claim that the SOD

\left\langle\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}(-H_{1}-E^{\prime})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-H_{2}-E^{\prime})\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{1}-E^{\prime})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{2}-E^{\prime})\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle\right\rangle

is mutation-equivalent to the SOD

\left\langle\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}(-H^{\prime}_{1})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-H^{\prime}_{2})\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}(-h^{\prime}_{1})\\
{\mathcal{O}}(-h^{\prime}_{2})\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle\right\rangle

which is pulled back from
X_{2}
and conclude the proof.
This can be shown as in the degree
5
case replacing the quiver
{\tilde{D}}_{4}
by the quiver
{\tilde{A}}_{3}
with alternating orientation (see Figure
1
).

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{1}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{2}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{3}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{4}^{\prime})}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}}(-H_{1}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{1}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{2}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}}(-H_{2}^{\prime})}

Figure 1.
Affine quivers
\tilde{D}_{4}
and
\tilde{A}_{3}

Degree of
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
is
8
.

The pull-backs of the standard atomic SOD-s from
X_{1},X_{2}
to
Z
are (where
X_{1},X_{2}
are Hirzebruch surfaces)

(A.35)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(Z)=\left\langle\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}_{E_{1}}(-1)\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{E_{n}}(-1)\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}(-s_{1}-h)\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}(-s_{1})\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}(-h)\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}\rangle\right\rangle,

(A.36)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(Z)=\left\langle\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}_{1}}(-1)\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}_{n}}(-1)\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}(-s_{2}-h)\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}(-s_{2})\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}(-h)\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}\rangle\right\rangle,

where
s_{1},s_{2}
are pull-backs of sections of
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
-bundles
X_{1},X_{2}\to{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
with
s_{i}^{2}\leq 0
.
Mutations (
A.25
) transform (
A.35
) into

(A.37)

\left\langle\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}_{1}}(-1)\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{E^{\prime}_{n}}(-1)\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{L}}_{1}\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{L}}_{2}\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}(-h)\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}\rangle\right\rangle,

where
{\mathcal{L}}_{1},{\mathcal{L}}_{2}
are some line bundles. Note that the category

\langle\langle{\mathcal{L}}_{1}\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{L}}_{2}\rangle\rangle=\langle\langle{\mathcal{O}}(-s_{2}-h)\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}(-s_{2})\rangle\rangle

is equivalent to
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1})
, so any two SOD-s of this category are mutations of each other, and hence (
A.36
) is a mutation of (
A.37
).

Links of type III.
These are inverse to links of type I, nothing to check anymore.

Links of type IV.
We need to check that if
X
has two Mori fibre space structures over curves
C_{1},C_{2}
then the two standard SOD-s
{{\mathcal{B}}^{\rm std}}(X/C_{1})
and
{{\mathcal{B}}^{\rm std}}(X/C_{2})
are mutation-equivalent.
By Proposition
2.10
, we have
\deg X\in\{1,2,4,8\}
and consider these cases.

Degree of
X
is
8
. Here
X\cong{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\times{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
, with the two fibrations denoted by
h_{1},h_{2}
. Then by the definition, the standard SOD-s are

\langle\langle{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{1}-h_{2})\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{2})\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{1})\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}\rangle\rangle

and

\langle\langle{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{1}-h_{2})\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{1})\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{2})\rangle,\langle{\mathcal{O}}\rangle\rangle,

which differ by the orthogonal mutation of the two middle terms.

Degree of
X
is
4
. The two
G
-equivariant fibrations
X\to{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
are given by divisor classes
h_{1},h_{2}
such that
h_{1}+h_{2}=-K_{X}
(cf. Lemma
2.23
), and the two standard SOD-s for
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(X)
are

\langle{\mathbf{A}}_{1},{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{1}),{\mathcal{O}}\rangle\quad\text{and}\quad\langle{\mathbf{A}}_{2},{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{2}),{\mathcal{O}}\rangle.

Applying mutations to the first one we get the second one:

\langle{\mathbf{A}}_{1},{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{1}),{\mathcal{O}}\rangle\overset{R_{1}}{\leadsto}\langle{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{1}),{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}_{1},{\mathcal{O}}\rangle\overset{K}{\leadsto}\langle{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}_{1},{\mathcal{O}},{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{1}-K)\rangle=\langle{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}_{1},{\mathcal{O}},{\mathcal{O}}(h_{2})\rangle\overset{L_{3}}{\leadsto}\\
\leadsto\langle{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}_{1},{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{2}),{\mathcal{O}}\rangle=\langle{\mathbf{A}}_{2},{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{2}),{\mathcal{O}}\rangle.

Degree of
X
is
1
or
2
. We treat the cases when
\deg X=1,2
uniformly and similarly to the cases of links of type II where the roof is a del Pezzo surface of degrees
1,2
. Let
\sigma
be the Bertini, respectively Geiser involution on
X
. Then
(see
[
39
, Th. 2.6]
)
the two
G
-equivariant fibrations
X\to{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
are given by divisor classes
h_{1},h_{2}
such that
\sigma(h_{1})=h_{2}
, and the two standard SOD-s for
{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(X)
are

\langle{\mathbf{A}}_{1},{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{1}),{\mathcal{O}}\rangle\quad\text{and}\quad\langle{\mathbf{A}}_{2},{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{2}),{\mathcal{O}}\rangle.

We use Lemma
3.17
as before. Assume
\deg X=2
, then the lemma says that
S_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\perp}}^{2}\cong\sigma^{*}[3]
, where
S_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\perp}}
is the Serre functor on
{\mathcal{O}}^{\perp}\subset{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(X)
.
Mutations
(L_{2})^{4}
take
\langle{\mathbf{A}}_{1},{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{1}),{\mathcal{O}}\rangle

to

\langle S_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\perp}}^{2}{\mathbf{A}}_{1},S_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\perp}}^{2}{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{1}),\langle{\mathcal{O}}\rangle\rangle=\langle\sigma^{*}{\mathbf{A}}_{1},\sigma^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{1}),\langle{\mathcal{O}}\rangle\rangle=\langle{\mathbf{A}}_{2},{\mathcal{O}}(-h_{2}),{\mathcal{O}}\rangle,

see (
3.4
).
For
\deg X=1
the argument is similar, it suffices to apply
(L_{2})^{6}
.

Proposition
4.25
is proven.
∎
